--- Commit Message ---
LU-19921 ptlrpc: add GSSIAM upcall operations and helper

This patch adds the upcall operations and tunable attributes
for the GSSIAM security flavor, along with the userspace helper
program (l_gssiam_upcall) and its manual page (l_gssiam_upcall.8).
The helper serves as a reference implementation that returns a
mock IAM token and passes it back to the kernel via a sysfs
downcall for testing and verification.

Specifically, this patch implements:

- UAPI Downcall Interface: Adds GSSIAM_DOWNCALL_MAGIC and
  struct gssiam_downcall_data to lustre_user.h, along with
  idd_token() and idd_principal() inline accessor helpers for
  kernel-to-userspace downcall communication.

- Upcall Cache API & Lifecycle:
  * Adds and exports upcall_cache_invalidate_all() in
    lustre/obdclass/upcall_cache.c and lustre/include/upcall_cache.h
    to invalidate all entries across an upcall cache instance.
  * Updates alloc_entry() in upcall_cache.c to inspect
    UC_CACHE_IS_INVALID() after calling uc_ops->init_entry(),
    immediately freeing the entry and returning NULL if
    initialization fails, allowing init_entry() callbacks to
    abort cache entry allocation.

- Kernel Upcall & Cache Operations: Implements the upcall and
  downcall parsing handlers (gssiam_do_upcall, gssiam_parse_downcall,
  gssiam_upcall_compare) in lustre/ptlrpc/gss/sec_gssiam.c.
  The upcall mechanism protects uc_upcall path lookups and
  call_usermodehelper() invocations under uc_upcall_rwsem,
  validates downcall payload string lengths against overflow,
  updates gssiam_sec_cache_key() to fall back to lgmi_loginuid
  for principal-less mounts, and supports sysfs cache flushing
  (by 64-bit key for individual entries or -1 for all entries via
  upcall_cache_invalidate_all).

- Userspace Helper Program: Adds lustre/utils/gss/l_gssiam_upcall.c
  to handle kernel sysfs upcalls, format GSSIAM downcall
  payloads, and return appropriate error codes on missing
  parameters or downcall open failures. Uses logmsg() for
  standardized logging across GSS tools.

- GSS Logging Refactoring: Moves __logmsg(), g_log_level,
  log_prefix, lgss_set_loglevel(), log_hex(), and log_hexl()
  from lgss_utils.c into err_util.c and err_util.h without
  behavior changes. This allows standalone GSS helper programs
  (such as l_gssiam_upcall and l_gssiam_auth) to call logmsg()
  without depending on liblustreapi.la or Kerberos/OpenSSL
  libraries, ensuring they build as native, installable ELF
  binaries without Libtool shell script wrappers.

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Mostly good, comments inline. The three new sptlrpc.gssiam tunables and the new l_gssiam_upcall binary are user-visible and reachable as soon as ptlrpc_gss loads, but nothing in lustre/tests exercises them even though Test-Parameters names sanity-sec. Could a sanity-sec case be added that sets/reads sptlrpc.gssiam.gssiam_upcall, feeds a malformed buffer to gssiam_downcall and expects EINVAL, and writes -1 to gssiam_flush? That would cover the parts that work today without needing the policy to be registered.

--- Message (Gerrit AI review for Lustre, 2026-08-06 02:32:46.000000000) ---
Patch Set 42:

(9 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Some concerns, see inline comments.

One whole-patch note: `gssiam_tunables_init()` runs unconditionally from `sptlrpc_gssiam_init()`, so `sptlrpc.gssiam.{gssiam_upcall,gssiam_downcall,gssiam_flush}` appear on every client that loads ptlrpc_gss. But `sptlrpc_gssiam_policy` is still not handed to `sptlrpc_register_policy()` (the XXX comment above), so `gssiam_refresh_token()` never runs and nothing ever calls `upcall_cache_get_entry()` on this cache. Every write to `gssiam_downcall` therefore falls into the "upcall for key %llu not expected" path in `upcall_cache_downcall()` and returns -EINVAL. Would it be cleaner to land the tunables in the same patch that registers the policy, so the exposed interface is functional when it appears?

Mostly good, minor comments inline. Nothing added here is covered by a test: gssiam_tunables_init() runs from the ptlrpc_gss module init, so sptlrpc.gssiam.gssiam_upcall/gssiam_downcall/gssiam_flush and the installed l_gssiam_upcall binary are all exercisable on a client today even with the policy still unregistered. Patch Set 65:

(5 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Mostly good, a few minor comments inline. The patch adds three new user-visible tunables (sptlrpc.gssiam.gssiam_upcall/gssiam_downcall/gssiam_flush) and an installed helper binary, but no test exercises them. Since l_gssiam_upcall is explicitly a mock reference implementation, a sanity-sec case that sets gssiam_upcall, triggers the round-trip and then flushes by key and by -1 would lock down the downcall payload layout and the flush semantics cheaply.

--- Message (Gerrit AI review for Lustre, 2026-08-28 15:45:40.000000000) ---
Patch Set 66:

(7 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Mostly good, minor comments inline. Nothing here looks like a correctness or memory-safety problem: the downcall payload bounds checks in gssiam_downcall_store() hold for every idd_token_len/idd_principal_len combination the sysfs write can produce, the stack buffers handed to call_usermodehelper() stay alive because UMH_WAIT_EXEC waits for the exec, and the new alloc_entry() early-free only affects gssiam (no other init_entry op sets UC_CACHE_INVALID).

The three new sptlrpc.gssiam.* tunables and the new installed binary are user-visible but nothing exercises them - would it be worth a small sanity-sec case that writes a bad magic and a bad length to gssiam_downcall and checks gssiam_flush=-1 is accepted? Those paths are reachable today even with the policy unregistered.
